-adically complete, it remains to see that the natural map

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\alpha}\prod_{k}\ell^{\alpha_{k}}\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{k})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\widehat{\bigoplus}_{k}\,\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{k})

is an isomorphism, or equivalently that the source of this map is
\ell
-adically complete. This can be checked on homotopy sheaves and by the usual arguments involving geometric realizations we reduce to showing this statement after replacing each
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{k})
by a completed direct sum of static compact objects. Then everything is static and the claimed isomorphism can easily be checked on sections
\Gamma(U,-)
for w-contractible
U
(reducing the problem to an easy problem about classical abelian groups).
∎

We are finally in the position to provide the promised characterization of nuclear sheaves in terms of trace-class maps. This will immediately enable us to prove v-descent as well, leading to our first main result on nuclear sheaves in this paper.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. A sheaf
\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
is called
basic nuclear
if it can be written as a sequential colimit
\mathcal{N}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
such that all
\mathcal{P}_{n}
are compact and all transition maps
\mathcal{P}_{n}\to\mathcal{P}_{n+1}
are trace-class.

Theorem 3.9
.

(i)

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Then for an
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaf
\mathcal{M}
on
X
the following are equivalent:

1.

\mathcal{M}
is nuclear.

2.

\mathcal{M}
is an
\omega_{1}
-filtered colimit of basic nuclear sheaves.

3.

For every compact
\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
the natural map
\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})\to\Hom(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})
is an isomorphism.

The
\infty
-category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated with the
\omega_{1}
-compact objects being precisely the basic nuclear sheaves. Moreover, this
\infty
-category contains all étale sheaves and is stable under all small colimits and under the tensor product in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
.

(ii)

The assignment

\displaystyle X\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

defines a hypercomplete sheaf of presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories on the v-site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

Proof.

We first prove (i), so let
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be given. The equivalence of (b) and (c) follows formally from
Lemma

3.6
, see
[
1
, Theorem 8.6]
. Now assume that
\mathcal{M}
is nuclear; we will show that it satisfies condition (c). Since (c) is stable under colimits in
\mathcal{M}
we can assume that
\mathcal{M}
is a Banach module. Then by
3.7.(ii)
both
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})
and
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})
are
\ell
-adically complete, so it is enough to show that
\mathcal{N}:=\mathcal{M}/\ell\mathcal{M}
satisfies (c). Now
\mathcal{N}
is étale, so by
2.5.(i)
we can write it as a colimit of copies of
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[U]
for varying basic
U\in X_{\mathrm{et}}
. It is therefore enough to show that
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[U]
satisfies (c), which follows by explicit computation (using that if we write
\mathcal{P}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
for qcqs étale sheaves
\mathcal{P}_{n}
then
\Hom(\mathcal{P},\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[U])=\varinjlim_{n}\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[U])
).

To finish the equivalence of (a), (b) and (c) it remains to show that (b) implies (a), so from now on assume that
\mathcal{M}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
is basic nuclear; we need to show that
\mathcal{M}
is nuclear. This follows if we can show that every map
\mathcal{P}_{n}\to\mathcal{P}_{n+1}
factors over some Banach sheaf because then
\mathcal{M}
is the colimit of these Banach sheaves. Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}
be the
\ell
-adic completion of the pushforward of
\mathcal{P}_{n+1}
to the étale site. We claim that the natural map

\displaystyle\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1})=\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{P}_{n+1})

is an isomorphism (here the first identity follows from (c) because
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}
is clearly nuclear), which provides the desired factorization
\mathcal{P}_{n}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}\to\mathcal{P}_{n+1}
. To prove this isomorphism, note that by
3.7.(ii)
both sides of the claimed isomorphism are
\ell
-adically complete, so we can check the isomorphism modulo
\ell
. Then it boils down to the following claim: Let
\mathcal{M}
(
=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}/\ell\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}
) be an
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
-module in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
and let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
denote its pushforward to the étale site. Then the natural map

\displaystyle\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}})=\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism (here the first identity follows because
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
is nuclear and hence satisfies (c) by the above). Both sides of the claimed isomorphism preserve all small colimits (for the functor
\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
this follows from the
\ell
-boundedness of
X
, as this implies that
\Gamma(U,-)
is cohomologically bounded for all basic
U\in X_{\mathrm{proet}}
), so we can assume that
\mathcal{M}
is compact. Then we have

\displaystyle\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M})=\varinjlim_{k}\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n,k},\mathcal{M})=\varinjlim_{k}\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n,k},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}})=\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}})

for any representation
\mathcal{P}_{n}=\varprojlim_{k}\mathcal{P}_{n,k}
with all
\mathcal{P}_{n,k}
being qcqs étale (in the first identity there is no
\ell
-adic completion required because all terms are killed by
\ell
). This finishes the proof that (a), (b) and (c) are equivalent.

We finish the proof of (i): It is formal that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated with the
\omega_{1}
-compact generators being precisely the basic nuclear objects (see
[
1
, Theorem 8.6]
). We have already shown that all étale sheaves are nuclear, and from (c) it follows immediately that nuclear sheaves are stable under all small colimits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
. To show that nuclear sheaves are also stable under tensor products, it is now enough to see this for basic nuclear objects, where it follows from
3.6.(ii)
(to get the require right-boundedness, note that the above
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{n+1}
are clearly bounded).

We now prove (ii). It is clear that nuclear sheaves are stable under pullback. As in the proof of
Corollary

2.7
, the claimed v-descent now reduces to the following claim: Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds and let
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
such that
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
is nuclear; then
\mathcal{M}
is nuclear. Let
f_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to X
be a hypercover of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds extending
f
. Then all
f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}
are nuclear. By
2.6.(ii)
the pushforwards
f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
preserve small colimits and Banach sheaves and thus nuclear sheaves, so that we deduce that all
f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}
are nuclear. Moreover, by v-descent for v-sheaves we have
\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}
. Fix any compact
\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
. Then by
3.7.(i)
the functor
\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},-)
is bounded and therefore preserves Postnikov limits and uniformly left-bounded totalizations. We deduce

\displaystyle\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{k}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\tau_{\leq k}\mathcal{M})=\varprojlim_{k}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\tau_{\leq k}\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{k}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\tau_{\leq k}\mathcal{M})=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\varprojlim_{k}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\tau_{\leq k}\mathcal{M}),

and since
f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
has finite cohomological dimension by
2.6.(ii)
we get

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\varprojlim_{k}f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\tau_{\leq k}\mathcal{M})=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}),

and finally by nuclearity of
f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\Hom(\mathcal{P},f_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}f_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M})=\Hom(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M}),

proving that
\mathcal{M}
is indeed nuclear.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

In the case that
X=\Spa(C)
is a geometric point, we recover the classical
\infty
-category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
of nuclear condensed
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules. In fact, for any profinite set
X
(viewed as a diamond over
\Spa(C)
) and all
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
with
\mathcal{P}
compact we have

\displaystyle\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{M})=\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{P},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\otimes\mathcal{M},

i.e. we recover the classical notion of nuclearity. Note that for non-compact
\mathcal{P}
the above identity is false, because by definition
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},-)
transforms colimits in
\mathcal{P}
into limits, whereas
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{P},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\otimes-
does not. This does not affect the notion of nuclear sheaves, because this only every uses
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},-)
for compact
\mathcal{P}
(some subtleties arise if compact objects are not stable under tensor products, though).

While many natural constructions of sheaves preserve the nuclear category, some (like internal hom) do not. We therefore need a way of “nuclearizing” sheaves, which can be done as follows.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. By the adjoint functor theorem the inclusion
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
admits a right adjoint

\displaystyle(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}),\qquad\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}}.

For every
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
we call
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}}
the
nuclearization
of
\mathcal{M}
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Then the nuclearization functor on
X
is characterized by the following properties:

(i)

The functor
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
preserves all small colimits.

(ii)

If
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
is
\ell
-adically complete, then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}}
is the
\ell
-adic completion of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
. In particular, if
\mathcal{M}
is killed by some power of
\ell
then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
.

(iii)

The functor
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
is bounded, i.e. there are integers
a\leq b
such that for every static
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
the sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}}
lies in homological degrees
[a,b]
(with respect to the solid
t
-structure).

Proof.

We construct a colimit-preserving functor

\displaystyle F\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

as follows: It is enough to construct it on compact objects and for compact
\mathcal{Q}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
we define
F(\mathcal{Q})
to be the
\ell
-adic completion of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{et}}
. After composing
F
with the inclusion
\iota\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
we get a natural map
\iota F\to\id
. It follows that there is a natural map
F\to(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
. We claim that this is an isomorphism. This can be checked on sections from basic nuclear objects, i.e. for every
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
and every sequential colimit
\mathcal{N}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
of compact
\mathcal{P}_{n}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
with trace-class transition maps, we need to verify that the natural map

\displaystyle\Hom(\mathcal{N},F(\mathcal{M}))\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Hom(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}})

is an isomorphism. Let us first compute the left-hand side. We claim that for any
\mathcal{L}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
the natural map

\displaystyle\Hom(\mathcal{L},\iota F(\mathcal{M}))=\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{L},\iota F(\mathcal{M}))\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism. This can be checked for compact
\mathcal{L}
; then both sides commute with colimits in
\mathcal{M}
, so we can also reduce to the case that
\mathcal{M}
is compact. Factoring out the
\ell
-adic completion on both sides, we can assume that
\mathcal{M}
is killed by
\ell
. But then both sides evaluate to
\varinjlim_{n}\Hom(\mathcal{L}_{n},\mathcal{M})
for any representation
\mathcal{L}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{L}_{n}
with qcqs étale
\mathcal{L}_{n}
.

Let us get back to the claimed isomorphism of
F
and
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
, so let
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
be as before. The claim now reduces to showing that the natural map

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M})=\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M})=\Hom(\mathcal{N},F(\mathcal{M}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Hom(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{nuc}})=\Hom(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M})=\varprojlim_{n}\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism. This follows by constructing sections
\Hom(\mathcal{P}_{n+1},\mathcal{M})\to\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{M})
, which exist by
3.6.(i)
.

Having established the isomorphism
F=(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
, we can now easily prove the claims (i)–(iii). Part (i) is obvious since
F
preserves all small colimits by construction. For part (ii) note that if
\mathcal{M}
is killed by
\ell
then clearly
F(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{et}}
. Since
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
preserves limits (as it is a right adjoint functor) we deduce that
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
preserves
\ell
-adically complete objects. For part (iii), we can immediately reduce to the case that
\mathcal{M}
is static and finitely presented and hence
\ell
-adically complete. Then the claim follows from the fact that both
\ell
-adic completion and pushforward to the étale site are bounded.
∎

We now have a good understanding of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-sheaves on
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds. It is formal to extend this notion to all small v-stacks and to modules over any nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
, where by “nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra” we mean an (animated condensed)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra whose underlying
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-module is nuclear. Prominent examples of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras are
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
,
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
,
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
and
\mathbb{C}_{\ell}
.

Definition 3.13
.

For any small v-stack
X
we define

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}\subseteq\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

by descent from the case of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds using
Corollaries

2.7
and
3.9.(ii)
(note that every strictly totally disconnected space is an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond, so that
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds form a basis for the v-site). In other words, a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
lies in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
if and only if this is true after pullback to every
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond.

Definition 3.14
.

Let
\Lambda
be a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra.

(a)

By pullback
\Lambda
defines a v-sheaf of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings on the big v-site of all small v-stacks, which we still denote
\Lambda
. In particular, for every small v-stack
X
, we get a stable
\infty
-category
\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)
of
\Lambda
-modules on
X
. This
\infty
-category admits a complete
t
-structure and the forgetful functor
\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is
t
-exact, conservative and preserves all small limits and colimits. Moreover, for any map of small v-stacks, the pullback and pushforward functors on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules can be enhanced to functors on
\Lambda
-modules.

(b)

Let
X
be a small v-stack. We define

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\subseteq\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)

to be the full subcategories spanned by those
\Lambda
-modules which lie in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
after applying the forgetful functor. Equivalently we can view
\Lambda
as an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
and define
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
as the
\infty
-category of
\Lambda
-modules in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
. The objects of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
are called the
nuclear
\Lambda
-modules on
X
and the objects of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
are called the
\omega_{1}
-solid
\Lambda
-modules on
X
.

We can formally extend most of the results on nuclear and
\omega_{1}
-solid
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules on
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds to nuclear and
\omega_{1}
-solid
\Lambda
-modules on small v-stacks:

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\Lambda
be a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra.

(i)

The assignment

\displaystyle X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}

defines a hypercomplete sheaf of presentable symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories with complete
t
-structure on the v-site of all small v-stacks.

(ii)

Let
X
be a small v-stack. The inclusion
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)
is
t
-exact and commutes with all pullbacks, colimits and countable limits.

(iii)

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Then
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is compactly generated. The compact objects are generated under finite (co)limits and retracts by the objects
\mathcal{P}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\Lambda
for compact
\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
.

Proof.

Part (i) is clear by definition for
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
(for presentability use that presentable
\infty
-categories are stable under limits and use (iii)). For general
\Lambda
it follows by repeating the argument in
Corollary

2.7
and noting that everything commutes with the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
. For part (ii) we can similarly reduce to the case
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. Then the commutation with pullbacks is true by design and the commutation with colimits and countable limits follows from
2.5.(iii)
because everything can be reduced to
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds (using that pullbacks preserve limits and colimits). Part (iii) follows easily from
2.5.(ii)
.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
\Lambda
be a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra.

(i)

The assignment

\displaystyle X\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

defines a hypercomplete sheaf of presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories on the v-site of all small v-stacks.

(ii)

Let
X
be a small v-stack. The inclusion
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is symmetric monoidal and commutes with all pullbacks and colimits.

(iii)

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated. The
\omega_{1}
-compact objects are generated under countable colimits by the objects
\mathcal{N}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\Lambda
for basic nuclear
\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.

Proof.

We can argue as in
Proposition

3.15
to reduce everything to
Theorem

3.9
.
∎

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
be a map of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras and let
X
be a small v-stack.

(i)

There is a natural pair of adjoint functors

\displaystyle-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime})\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{Forget},

both of which commute with all pullbacks and colimits. Moreover, the functor
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
is symmetric monoidal and the forgetful functor is conservative and preserves all limits.

The same is true for the
\infty
-categories of
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves instead of nuclear sheaves, in which case the forgetful functor is additionally
t
-exact.

(ii)

Both
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
and the forgetful functor commute with the inclusion of nuclear sheaves into
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is the
\infty
-category of
\Lambda
-modules in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
(and similarly for
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
) and the fact that pullback functors and the inclusion of nuclear sheaves into
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves are symmetric monoidal.
∎

It is convenient to also define the nuclearization functor in the general setting of nuclear
\Lambda
-modules on small v-stacks, even though on general small v-stacks it will not have the same nice properties.

Definition 3.18
.

Let
\Lambda
be a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra and
X
a small v-stack. We define the
nuclearization
functor

\displaystyle(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

to be the right adjoint of the inclusion.

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
\Lambda
be a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra and let
X
be a small v-stack.

(i)

If
X
is an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond then the functor
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
preserves all small colimits and is bounded with respect to the
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(ii)

If
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
is a map of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras then nuclearization on
X
commutes with the forgetful functor along
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Part (ii) follows immediately from
3.17.(ii)
by passing to right adjoints. Part (i) follows from (ii) and
Proposition

3.12
(use the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
).
∎

In the case that the nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
is discrete (e.g.
\Lambda=\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
), we recover the classical theory of étale
\Lambda
-modules:

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
\Lambda
be a discrete
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra and
X
a small v-stack. Then we have

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)=\D_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\Lambda)

as full subcategories of
\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Since both
\infty
-categories are defined by descent, we can assume that
X
is an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. By
3.9.(i)
every étale
\Lambda
-module is nuclear (for any nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
), so we have
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
. To get the other inclusion, note that the discreteness of
\Lambda
implies that
\Hom({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},\Lambda)=\varinjlim_{n}\Hom({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}},\Lambda)
(a priori this holds in the
\infty
-category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules, but then also follows in the
\infty
-category of rings). Applying this to the structure map
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
we deduce that
\Lambda
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}
-algebra for some
n
. In particular every
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is killed by
\ell^{n}
, which implies that
\mathcal{M}
is étale by
3.12.(ii)
.
∎

4
Pushforward and Base-Change

Fix a prime
\ell\neq p
and a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
. In the previous section we have constructed the
\infty
-category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
of nuclear
\Lambda
-modules on every small v-stack
X
. We will now introduce the pushforward of nuclear sheaves and study its behavior.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of small v-stacks.

(a)

We denote the pullback functor on nuclear sheaves by

\displaystyle f^{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda).

It is symmetric monoidal, preserves all small colimits and coincides with the v-pullback by design.

(b)

We denote the
nuclear pushforward
functor by

\displaystyle f_{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda).

It is defined to be the right adjoint of
f^{*}
, which exists by the adjoint functor theorem. It may occasionally be necessary to also consider pushforward functors on
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves and on all v-sheaves (defined to be the right adjoints of the pullback functor on the respective
\infty
-categories), which we will denote
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
and
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}
, respectively.

In general we cannot expect the nuclear pushforward to coincide with the v-pushforward because the latter will in general not preserve nuclear sheaves. However, this usually works under a qcqs assumption, as follows.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
X
be a small v-stack.

(a)

We denote by

\displaystyle\D^{b}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda),\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda),\D^{-}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

the full subcategories of those v-sheaves whose pullback to every
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond is bounded, resp. left bounded, resp. right bounded with respect to the
t
-structure on
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves. The elements of these subcategories are called the
locally bounded
, resp.
locally left-bounded
, resp.
locally right-bounded
nuclear
\Lambda
-modules on
X
.

(b)

Similar definitions as in (a) apply to
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves and to v-sheaves in place of nuclear sheaves.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
?\in\{b,+,-\}
the assignment
X\mapsto\D^{?}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
defines a hypercomplete sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories on the v-site of all small v-stacks.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that pullbacks are
t
-exact and that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)
is a hypercomplete v-sheaf.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

We warn the reader that there is no
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
(unless
\Lambda
is discrete, see
Proposition

3.20
), but it still makes sense to speak of (left/right) bounded nuclear sheaves.

We can now prove the following characterizations of the nuclear pushforward, generalizing the ones in
[
15
, Proposition 17.6]
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a qcqs map of small v-stacks.

(i)

The v-pushforward
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}\colon\D(Y_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{v}},\Lambda)
preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves, i.e. we have
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}=f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
.

(ii)

The v-pushforward preserves locally left-bounded nuclear sheaves, so that we have
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}=f_{*}
on locally left-bounded sheaves.

(iii)

Suppose that the functor
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
has finite cohomological dimension. Then (ii) also hold for unbounded sheaves. Moreover, in this case both
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
and
f_{*}
preserve all small colimits.

Proof.

All statements can be checked after pullback to any v-cover, so we can assume that
X
is an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond; in particular
Y
is qcqs. Moreover, since the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
commutes with pushforwards we can further reduce to the case that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. We fix a hypercover
g_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to Y
by
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds, so that
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}
is computed as the totalization
f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}f_{n{\mathrm{v}}*}g_{n}^{*}
.

Part (i) now follows from that fact that all
f_{n{\mathrm{v}}*}
preserve
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves (see
2.6.(ii)
and that
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves on
X
are stable under countable limits (see
2.5.(iii)
). Part (ii) follows by the same argument as in the proof of
3.9.(ii)
by exploiting the fact that
\Hom^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathcal{P},-)
preserves uniformly left-bounded totalizations. Part (iii) follows similarly by additionally taking limits over Postnikov truncations (the fact that
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
and
f_{*}
commute with colimits follows easily from the facts that
f
is qcqs and has bounded cohomological dimension by the usual Postnikov limit argument).
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

be a cartesian diagram of small v-stacks and assume that
f
is qcqs.

(i)

The natural morphism
g^{*}f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}f^{\prime}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}g^{\prime*}
is an isomorphism of functors
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(ii)

The natural morphism
g^{*}f_{*}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}
is an isomorphism of functors
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)
.

(iii)

Suppose that the functor
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
has finite cohomological dimension. Then (ii) also holds for unbounded sheaves.

Proof.

All claims follows immediately from
Proposition

4.5
by using the fact that v-pushforward satisfies arbitrary base-change (for (iii) note additionally that the functor
f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{v}}*}g^{\prime*}
preserves nuclear sheaves because so does
g^{*}f_{{\mathrm{v}}*}
).
∎

The condition that
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
has finite cohomological dimension may look a bit hard to grasp at first, but it turns out that this condition is usually satisfied in practice:

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of small v-stacks which is locally compactifiable, representable in spatial diamonds and has locally finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
. Then
f_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
has finite cohomological dimension.

Proof.

By
4.6.(i)
we can assume that
X
is an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond; in particular
Y
is also a spatial diamond. By passing to a finite cover of
Y
we can assume that
f
is compactifiable. Then
[
15
, Theorem 22.5]
implies that
f_{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
has finite cohomological dimension, which implies that
Y
is
\ell
-bounded. We conclude by
2.6.(ii)
.
∎

5
The 6-Functor Formalism

Fix a prime
\ell\neq p
and a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
. We will finally construct the 6-functor formalism for nuclear
\Lambda
-sheaves. In the previous subsection we have already introduced the pullback and pushforward functors. The next pair of functors are tensor product and internal
\Hom
, which are easily defined:

Definition 5.1
.

Let
X
be a small v-stack. We denote by
\otimes_{\Lambda}
the symmetric monoidal structure on
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
and by
\IHom_{\Lambda}(-,-)
the associated internal hom functor (which exists because
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is presentable). We often drop the subscript
\Lambda
if there is no room for confusion.

The last pair of functors – the shriek functors – are the hardest to construct. The general idea is as follows: Given a nice compactifiable map
f\colon Y\to X
of small v-stacks we want to define the functor
f_{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
as the composition
f_{!}=g_{*}\mathbin{\circ}j_{!}
for any decomposition of
f
into an open immersion
j
and a proper map
g
; here
j_{!}
will be left adjoint of
j^{*}
.

As a first step towards constructing
f_{!}
, let us show that
j_{!}
exists and satisfies the expected properties:

Lemma 5.2
.

For every étale map
j\colon U\to X
of small v-stacks the pullback
j^{*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
admits a left adjoint

\displaystyle j_{!}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}

with the following properties:

(i)

For every map
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of small v-stacks with base-change
g^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}:=U\times_{X}X^{\prime}\to U
,
j^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
the natural morphism

\displaystyle j^{\prime}_{!}g^{\prime*}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}g^{*}j_{!}

is an isomorphism of functors
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(ii)

For all
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
and
\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
the natural map

\displaystyle j_{!}(\mathcal{N}\otimes j^{*}\mathcal{M})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}j_{!}\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{M}

is an isomorphism.

(iii)

j_{!}
commutes with the forgetful functor and the base-change functor along any map
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras.

(iv)

If
j
is quasicompact then
j_{!}
is
t
-exact and preserves
\ell
-adically complete sheaves.

(v)

j_{!}
preserves nuclear sheaves and hence restricts to a functor

\displaystyle j_{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(U,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

which is left adjoint to
j^{*}
.

Proof.

By
[
3
, Proposition VII.3.1]
the pullback functor
j^{*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,\Lambda)
admits a left adjoint
j_{\natural}
which satisfies the analogous properties (i), (ii) and (iii) (the result in loc. cit. is only stated for static
\Lambda
but the proof works in general; in fact, the proof is completely formal). We now show that
j_{\natural}
preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves and hence restricts to the desired functor
j_{!}
. Since
j_{\natural}
commutes with the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
, we can assume that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. Moreover, since
j_{\natural}
commutes with any base-change, we can assume that
X
is a strictly totally disconnected space. Then
U
is a perfectoid space and hence admits a basis given by open subsets which are quasicompact and separated over
X
(e.g. take affinoid perfectoid open subsets). It is formal that
j_{\natural}
is computed as the colimit over this basis, so we can assume that
j
is quasicompact and separated. In particular
U
is strictly totally disconnected and hence an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Since
j_{\natural}
preserves all small colimits, it is now enough to verify that for every compact
\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
we have
j_{\natural}\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
. By
2.5.(ii)
we can assume that
\mathcal{P}={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V]
for some countable basic
V=\varprojlim_{n}V_{n}\in X_{\mathrm{proet}}
. But then for all
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
we have

\displaystyle\Hom(j_{\natural}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V],\mathcal{M})=\Hom({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V],j^{*}\mathcal{M})=\Gamma(V,j^{*}\mathcal{M})=\Gamma(V,\mathcal{M}),

hence
j_{\natural}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V]={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V]
(where on the right-hand side we define it as an sheaf on
X
), which is indeed
\omega_{1}
-solid.

We have now shown the existence of
j_{!}
by restricting
j_{\natural}
to
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves. The claims (i), (ii) and (iii) now follow immediately from the analogous properties of
j_{\natural}
. It remains to prove (iv) and (v), for which we can assume that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
(by (iii)), that
X
is strictly totally disconnected (by (i)) and that
j
is quasicompact and separated (by passing to an open cover as above).

We first observe that
j_{!}
preserves étale sheaves: By passing to right adjoints, this reduces to the observation that
j^{*}
commutes with the pushforward to the étale site, which follows immediately from the fact that
j
is étale. Therefore claim (v) reduces to claim (iv), because every nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-sheaf on
U
is a colimit of Banach sheaves. To prove (iv) we need to make one more computation: Suppose
\mathcal{P}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
is static and can be written as a sequential limit
\mathcal{P}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}
with static qcqs étale
\mathcal{P}_{n}\in\D_{\mathrm{et}}(U,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{\omega}
; then

\displaystyle j_{!}\mathcal{P}=\varprojlim_{n}j_{!}\mathcal{P}_{n}.

(5.2.1)

To prove this, pick a surjective map
\mathcal{Q}_{0}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
for some static compact
\omega_{1}
-solid
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-module
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
on
U
. We can write
\mathcal{Q}_{0}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{Q}_{0,n}
for some qcqs étale sheaves
\mathcal{Q}_{0,n}
and by the usual Breen resolution argument the map
\mathcal{Q}_{0}\to\mathcal{P}
can be obtained from a map of
\Pro
-systems
(\mathcal{Q}_{0,n})_{n}\to(\mathcal{P}_{n})_{n}
. In particular the kernel of the map
\mathcal{Q}_{0}\to\mathcal{P}
is again a limit of qcqs étale sheaves, so we can iterate the process in order to obtain a resolution of the
\Pro
-system
(\mathcal{P}_{n})_{n}
in terms of
\Pro
-systems
(\mathcal{Q}_{k,n})_{n}
of static qcqs étale sheaves such that each
\mathcal{Q}_{k}:=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{Q}_{k,n}
is compact. By passing to the associated simplicial objects (whose geometric realization is
\mathcal{P}
) and using that both
j_{!}
and countable limits commute with geometric realizations, we reduce the claim
Eq.

5.2.1
to the case that
(\mathcal{P}_{n})_{n}=(\mathcal{Q}_{k,n})_{n}
for some
k
. But since
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
is compact, the claim follows from the above computation of
j_{!}
on compact generators.

With
Eq.

5.2.1
at hand, claim (iv) is now straightforward: Note that it follows immediately that
j_{!}
is
t
-exact, because every static
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaf can be written as a filtered colimits of objects of the form
\mathcal{P}
in
Eq.

5.2.1
(use also
[
3
, Proposition VII.1.6]
). Thus to show that
j_{!}
preserves
\ell
-adically complete sheaves, we can immediately reduce to the static case. From now on let
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
be static and
\ell
-adically complete. To show that
j_{!}\mathcal{M}
is
\ell
-adically complete, we can as in the proof of
Proposition

2.8
pass to a geometric realization of
\mathcal{M}
in order to reduce to the case that
\mathcal{M}=\widehat{\bigoplus}_{k}\,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V_{k}]
for countably many basic
V_{k}=\varprojlim_{n}V_{k,n}\in U_{\mathrm{proet}}
(here we use that
j_{!}
preserves colimits and is right
t
-exact). Now filter
\mathcal{M}
by the subsheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}=\prod_{k}\ell^{\alpha_{k}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V_{k}]
for sequences of integers
\alpha_{k}\geq 0
converging to
\infty
. By
Eq.

5.2.1
we get

\displaystyle j_{!}\mathcal{M}=\varinjlim_{\alpha}j_{!}\prod_{k}\ell^{\alpha_{k}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V_{k}]=\varinjlim_{\alpha}\prod_{k}j_{!}(\ell^{\alpha_{k}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V_{k}])=\widehat{\bigoplus}_{k}\,\ell^{\alpha_{k}}j_{!}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[V_{k}]),

as desired.
∎

Having a good understanding of
j_{!}
for étale
j
, it remains to study
g_{*}
for nice proper maps
g
. We already know base-change for
g_{*}
by
4.6.(iii)
. It remains to check the projection formula. Also, in order to show that the construction
f_{!}=g_{*}\mathbin{\circ}j_{!}
is independent of the factorization, we need a compatibility of
g_{*}
and
j_{!}
:

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
g\colon Y\to X
be a proper map of small v-stacks which has locally bounded dimension (see
[
10
, Definition 3.5.3]
) and assume that the functor
g_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
has finite cohomological dimension. Then:

(i)

For all
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)
the natural map

\displaystyle g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{M}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}g_{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Let
j\colon U\to X
be an open immersion with base-change
j^{\prime}\colon V\to Y
and
g^{\prime}\colon V\to U
. Then the natural map

\displaystyle j_{!}g^{\prime}_{*}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}g_{*}j^{\prime}_{!}

is an isomorphism of functors
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(V,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Part (ii) follows formally from part (i) together with
Lemma

5.2
and
4.6.(iii)
, see
[
10
, Lemma 3.6.8]
. To prove (i), we note that both sides of the claimed isomorphism commute with base-change (by
4.6.(iii)
), so we can assume that
X
is a strictly totally disconnected space. Moreover, both sides of the claimed isomorphism also commute with colimits in
\mathcal{M}
, so we can assume
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\Lambda
for some basic nuclear
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
(see
3.16.(iii)
). But then after applying the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
(and using that
g_{*}
commutes with this forgetful functor) we get

\displaystyle g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\Lambda}(\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})=g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime},

\displaystyle g_{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\Lambda}g^{*}\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=g_{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\Lambda}(\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}g^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}))=g_{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}g^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}).

We can therefore assume that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. Now write
\mathcal{M}=\varinjlim_{i}\mathcal{P}_{i}
for compact objects
\mathcal{P}_{i}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
. Then by
3.12.(i)
we have
\mathcal{M}=\varinjlim_{i}(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{\mathrm{nuc}}
and by the other claims in
Proposition

3.12
each
(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{\mathrm{nuc}}
is a bounded Banach sheaf. By pulling out colimits on both sides of the claimed isomorphism we can thus reduce to the case that
\mathcal{M}
is a bounded Banach sheaf.

We will first prove the claim in the case that
Y
is a spatial diamond (it is automatically
\ell
-bounded by
[
15
, Theorem 22.5]
). Then, since both sides of the claimed isomorphism commute with colimits in
\mathcal{N}
(for
g_{*}
this was shown in
4.5.(iii)
), we can use the same strategy as for
\mathcal{M}
in order to reduce to the case that
\mathcal{N}
is also a bounded Banach sheaf. Now by
Proposition

2.8
both sides of the claimed isomorphism are
\ell
-adically complete, hence we can check the isomorphism after reducing modulo
\ell
. But then the claim follows immediately from
[
15
, Proposition 22.11]
.

To prove the claim for general
Y
, pick a hypercover
h_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to Y
such that all
Y_{n}
are spatial diamonds and all maps
g_{n}\colon Y_{n}\to X
are proper and of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
(e.g. start with any hypercover of
Y
by affinoid perfectoid spaces of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
over
X
and then take relative compactifications). Note that the functor

\displaystyle F\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}},\qquad\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mapsto g_{*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\otimes\mathcal{M}

is bounded. Indeed, it follows formally from adjunctions that
g_{*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}=(g_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{nuc}}
, so it is enough to see that the functors
g_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
,
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}
(on
X
) and
-\otimes\mathcal{M}
are bounded. For the first functor this holds by assumption, for the second functor this was shown in
3.12.(iii)
and for the third functor it follows from the boundedness of
\mathcal{M}
together with
[
3
, Proposition VII.2.3]
. By a similar argument one sees that the composition of functors
F\mathbin{\circ}h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}
is bounded (this boils down to the functor
g_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}
having finite cohomological dimension). Thus by taking Postnikov limits and commuting them with totalizations, we deduce that
F(\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime})=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}F(h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime})
for all
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
. Taking
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}
we get

\displaystyle g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=F(\mathcal{N})=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}(g_{n*}h_{n}^{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{M}),

and using the fact that we already know that the projection formula holds for each
g_{n}
in place of
g
by the above argument,

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}g_{n*}(h_{n}^{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes g_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M})=g_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{M}).

But by v-descent for
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves we have
\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
for every
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
. By applying this to
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{M}
we conclude

\displaystyle g_{*}\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=g_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}h_{n{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}*}h_{n}^{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{M})=g_{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{M}),

as desired.
∎

With the above results at hand, it is now formal to construct the 6-functor formalism. We first construct it for maps which are representable in locally spatial diamonds and afterwards extend it to certain “stacky” maps. In the case of locally spatial diamonds, we will define shriek functors for the following class of maps:

Definition 5.4
.

A map
f\colon Y\to X
of small v-stacks is called
fdcs
1
1

1

The name comes from “Finite Dimension, Compactifiable and Spatial”.
if it is locally compactifiable (i.e. there is some analytic cover of
Y
on which
f
is compactifiable) and representable in locally spatial diamonds and has locally finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

(i)

The property of being fdcs is analytically local on both source and target.

(ii)

Fdcs maps are stable under composition and base-change.

(iii)

Every étale map is fdcs.

(iv)

Let
f\colon Y\to X
and
g\colon Z\to Y
be maps of small v-stacks. If
f
and
f\mathbin{\circ}g
are fdcs then so is
g
.

Proof.

Claims (i), (ii) and (iii) are obvious (using
[
15
, Proposition 22.3]
to handle the compactifiability condition). For (iv) we can argue in the same way as for the similar bdcs condition, see
[
10
, Lemma 3.6.10.(iv)]
.
∎

The next result constructs the 6-functor formalism for fdcs maps. The result will freely make use of the theory of abstract 6-functor formalisms developed in
[
10
, §A.5]
, so the reader is advised to take a look at that. In particular, recall the definition of the
\infty
-operad
\Corr(\mathcal{C})_{E,all}
of correspondences and of 6-functor formalisms, see
[
10
, Definitions A.5.2.(b), A.5.4, A.5.7]
. Also, in the following we will denote by
\vStacks
the 2-category of small v-stacks.

Proposition 5.6
.

There is a 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{fdcs,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

with the following properties:

(i)

Restricted to the symmetric monoidal subcategory
\vStacks^{\mathrm{op}}
(equipped with the coproduct monoidal structure),
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)
coincides with the functor constructed in
3.16.(i)
.

(ii)

For every fdcs map
f\colon Y\to X
the functor

\displaystyle f_{!}:=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}([Y\mathbin{\xleftarrow{\id}}Y\mathbin{\xrightarrow{f}}X],\Lambda)\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

preserves all small colimits. If
f=j
is étale then
j_{!}
is left adjoint to
j^{*}
and if
f
is proper then
f_{!}=f_{*}
.

Proof.

The construction is very similar to
[
10
, Theorem 3.6.12]
; in fact, we copied most of the proof with only minor adjustments. The most prominent difference is that we make use of the notion of prespatial diamonds from
[
4
]
to get a replacement for
p
-boundedness.

We first construct the 6-functor formalism for morphisms in the collection
fdcqc
of those maps
f\colon Y\to X
which are representable in prespatial diamonds (see
[
4
, Definition 3.3]
), compactifiable and quasicompact and have finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
. One checks that the class of maps
fdcqc
satisfies the “2-out-of-3 property” (i.e. the analog og
5.5.(iv)
) by a similar argument as in the proof of
5.5.(iv)
(note that it follows easily from the definition of prespatial diamonds that a quasicompact subdiamond of a prespatial diamond is prespatial). We denote by
P\subseteq fdcqc
the subclass of those maps which are additionally proper and by
I\subseteq fdcqc
the class of quasicompact open immersions. It follows easily from the 2-out-of-3 property for
fdcqc
that both
P
and
I
also have the 2-out-of-3 property. Thus by
[
4
, Proposition 3.6]
the pair
I,P\subseteq fdcqc
is a suitable decomposition in the sense of
[
10
, Definition A.5.9]
. We can therefore apply
[
10
, Proposition A.5.10]
: Condition (a) follows from
Lemma

5.2
, condition (b) follows from
4.6.(iii)
and
5.3.(i)
using
[
4
, Proposition 3.7]
(applied as in
Proposition

4.7
), condition (c) follows from
5.3.(ii)
, conditions (d) and (e) are clear because
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is presentable and condition (f) reduces to the observation that for
f\in P
the functor
f_{*}
preserves small colimits by
4.5.(iii)
. We obtain a 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{qc}\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{fdcqc,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

mapping
X\in\vStacks
to
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
with the correct pullback functors. We will now extend
\D_{qc}
from
fdcqc
to
fdcs
by using the extension results in
[
10
, §A.5]
.

1.

In the first step, we first restrict
\D_{qc}
to
\Corr(\vStacks)_{fdcsqc,all}
, where
fdcsqc\subseteq fdcqc
is the subset of those edges which are representable in spatial diamonds. We now wish to extend
\D_{qc}
from
fdcsqc
to the class of edges
fdcss
consiting of those fdcs maps which are separated. By
[
10
, Proposition A.5.16]
and
3.16.(i)
this extension can be performed on the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{1}\subseteq\vStacks
consisting of separated locally spatial diamonds, i.e. we need to extend the
\infty
-operad map

\displaystyle\D_{qc}\colon\Corr(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{fdcsqc,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

to an
\infty
-operad map

\displaystyle\D_{s}\colon\Corr(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{fdcss,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}.

We first apply
[
10
, Proposition A.5.12]
, which allows us to extend
\D_{qc}
from
fdcsqc
to the collection
E_{1}
of edges of the form
\bigsqcup_{i}Y_{i}\to X
, where each
Y_{i}\to X
lies in
fdcsqc
; let us denote the new 6-functor formalism by
\D^{\prime}_{qc}
. We now apply
[
10
, Proposition A.5.14]
to extend
\D=\D^{\prime}_{qc}
from
E_{1}
to
E^{\prime}_{1}:=fdcss
. Here we use the collection
S_{1}\subseteq E_{1}
of edges of the form
\bigsqcup_{i}U_{i}\to X
for covers
X=\bigcup_{i}U_{i}
by quasicompact open immersions
U_{i}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}X
. Then for
j\in S_{1}
we have
j^{!}=j^{*}
, hence condition (b) of loc. cit. follows from the sheafiness of
\D(-)
. Condition (c) amounts to saying that every separated fdcs map
Y\to X
of separated locally spatial diamonds admits a cover
Y=\bigcup_{i}V_{i}
by quasicompact open immersions
V_{i}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}Y
such that each map
V_{i}\to X
is quasicompact (it is automatically compactifiable by
[
15
, Proposition 22.3.(v)]
); but this is easily satisfied, e.g. pick the
V_{i}
to be any open cover of
Y
by quasicompact open subsets (then the maps
V_{i}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}Y
and
V_{i}\to X
are quasicompact because both
X
and
Y
are separated). Finally, condition (d) follows easily from the fact that all the spaces in
\mathcal{C}_{1}
are separated. This finishes the construction of the 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{s}\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{fdcss,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

(where we implicitly used
[
10
, Proposition A.5.16]
to extend from
\mathcal{C}_{1}
to
\vStacks
).

2.

In the second extension step we extend
\D_{s}
to the desired
\infty
-operad map

\displaystyle\D\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{fdcs,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}.

This extension is similar to the previous one, albeit somewhat simpler: We can perform the extension directly on
\mathcal{C}_{2}=\vStacks
by applying
[
10
, Proposition A.5.14]
to
E_{2}=fdcss
and
E_{2}^{\prime}=fdcs
with
S_{2}\subseteq E_{2}
being the collection of all open immersions.

We have now constructed a 6-functor formalism
\D(-,\Lambda)=\D
on fdcs maps. It remains to verify that it satisfies claims (i) and (ii). Claim (i) is obvious from the construction. For claim (ii), it follows from the definition of 6-functor formalisms that
f_{!}
preserves all small colimits and it follows immediately from the construction that
f_{!}=f_{*}
for proper
f
. It remains to see that for every étale map
j\colon U\to X
of small v-stacks the just constructed functor
j_{!}
is left adjoint to
j^{*}
and thus coincides with the functor
j_{!}
from
Lemma

5.2
. To see this, we first apply
[
10
, Proposition A.5.10]
to the case that
E=I=et
is the collection of étale maps in
\vStacksCoeff
and
P
consists only of degenerate edges; then conditions (b) and (c) are vacuous and condition (a) is satisfied by
Lemma

5.2
. We thus obtain a 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{et}}\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{et,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty},\qquad X\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda).

We need to show that
\D_{\mathrm{et}}
is equivalent to the restriction of
\D
to
\Corr(\vStacks)_{et,all}
. By the uniqueness of the extension results
[
10
, Proposition A.5.12, A.5.14, A.5.16]
we can show this equivalence on the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\vStacks
consisting of locally spatial diamonds and we can then restrict to the subset
etsqc\subseteq et
of separated quasicompact étale maps. We can now further reduce to the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
consisting of strictly totally disconnected spaces. But note that every map of strictly totally disconnected spaces which lies in
etsqc
is of the form
\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\to X
for quasicompact open immersions
U_{i}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}X
, so we can further replace
etsqc
by the collection of quasicompact open immersions. But in this case
\D_{\mathrm{et}}
and
\D
agree by construction.
∎

We now want to extend the 6-functor formalism from
Proposition

5.6
to certain “stacky” maps. The relevant definitions are as follows:

Definition 5.7
.

We say that an fdcs map
f\colon Y\to X
of small v-stacks admits
universal
\ell
-codescent
if it satisfies the following property: Given any small v-stack
X^{\prime}
which admits a map to some strictly totally disconnected space and given any map
X^{\prime}\to X
with base-change
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
and Čech nerve
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\to X^{\prime}
the natural functor

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(X^{\prime},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(Y^{\prime}_{n},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

is an equivalence. Here
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(-,\Lambda)
denotes the functor
Z\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Z,\Lambda)
,
h\mapsto h^{!}
obtained from the 6-functor formalism in
Proposition

5.6
.

Definition 5.8
.

A map
f\colon Y\to X
of small v-stacks is called
\ell
-fine
if there is a map
g\colon Z\to Y
such that
g
and
f\mathbin{\circ}g
are fdcs and
g
admits universal
\ell
-codescent.

Remark 5.9
.

There is a definition of
fine
maps in
[
4
, Definition 1.3.i]
which is closely related to our definition of
\ell
-fine maps. In fact, every fine map is
\ell
-fine, as follows from
Corollary

8.9
below. Moreover, we introduced a similar notion of
p
-fine
maps in
[
5
, Definition 2.4]
which only takes into account the universal codescent for
p
-
torsion
coefficients – this seems to be a mistake, as it is probably not enough to imply universal codescent for
p
-adic non-torsion coefficients (once the corresponding 6-functor formalism has been worked out).

Lemma 5.10
.

(i)

The condition of being
\ell
-fine is étale local on source and target.

(ii)

The collection of
\ell
-fine maps is stable under composition and base-change.

(iii)

Every fdcs map is
\ell
-fine.

(iv)

Let
f\colon Y\to X
and
g\colon Z\to Y
be maps of small v-stacks. If
f
and
f\mathbin{\circ}g
are
\ell
-fine then so is
g
.

Proof.

This is formal, see
[
5
, Lemma 2.5]
for a
p
-torsion analog (and use
5.5.(iv)
in place of
[
10
, Lemma 3.6.10.(iv)]
).
∎

In
Sections

8
and
10
we will provide many examples of
\ell
-fine maps which are not fdcs (and usually not
0
-truncated). We can finally formulate the main result of this paper:

Theorem 5.11
.

There is a 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)\colon\Corr(\vStacks)_{lfine,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

with the following properties:

(i)

Restricted to the symmetric monoidal subcategory
\vStacks^{\mathrm{op}}
(equipped with the coproduct monoidal structure),
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)
coincides with the functor constructed in
3.16.(i)
.

(ii)

For every
\ell
-fine map
f\colon Y\to X
the functor

\displaystyle f_{!}:=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}([Y\mathbin{\xleftarrow{\id}}Y\mathbin{\xrightarrow{f}}X],\Lambda)\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

preserves all small colimits. If
f=j
is étale then
j_{!}
is left adjoint to
j^{*}
and if
f
is proper then
f_{!}=f_{*}
.

Proof.

We first prove the following claim: Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of small v-stacks with Čech nerve
f_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to X
and assume that
f
admits universal
\ell
-codescent and
X
admits a map to some strictly totally disconnected space; then the natural functor

\displaystyle f_{\bullet}^{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(X,\Lambda)\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(Y_{n},\Lambda)

is an equivalence. By definition of universal
\ell
-codescent this holds for
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
, so we only need to reduce the general case to this case. Note that the functor
f_{\bullet}^{!}
has a left adjoint

\displaystyle f_{\bullet!}\colon\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(Y_{n},\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(X,\Lambda),\qquad(\mathcal{M}_{n})_{n}\mapsto\varinjlim_{n\in\Delta}f_{n!}\mathcal{M}_{n}.

Thus in order to prove the desired equivalence it is enough to show that the natural maps
f_{\bullet!}f_{\bullet}^{!}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\id
and
\id\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}f_{\bullet}^{!}f_{\bullet!}
are isomorphisms. This follows immediately from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-case together with the fact that both functors commute with the forgetful functor along
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Lambda
, the proof of which we delay to the end of this section (see
Corollary

5.15
below).

With the above claim at hand, the construction of the desired 6-functor formalism for
\ell
-fine maps is now completely formal (cf. the proof of
[
5
, Proposition 2.6]
): Our goal is to show that the 6-functor formalism from
Proposition

5.6
extends uniquely to a 6-functor formalism for all
\ell
-fine maps. To do that, let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\vStacks
denote the full subcategory spanned by those small v-stacks which admit a map to some strictly totally disconnected space. Then
\mathcal{C}
is a basis of
\vStacks
, hence by
[
10
, Proposition A.5.16]
it is enough to construct the desired extension of the 6-functor formalism on
\mathcal{C}
, i.e. we need to construct a 6-functor formalism
\D(-,\Lambda)\colon\Corr(\mathcal{C})_{lfine,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}
extending the one from
Proposition

5.6
. We now apply
[
10
, Proposition A.5.14]
with
E=fdcs
,
E^{\prime}=lfine
and
S\subseteq E
being the subset of those maps which admit universal
\ell
-codescent. Condition (a) is clear, condition (b) was proved above, condition (c) holds by definition of
\ell
-fine maps and condition (d) follows from
Lemma

5.5
(see
[
10
, Remark A.5.15.(ii)]
).
∎

Let us extract the shriek functors from
Theorem

5.11
, thereby completing the collection of six functors for nuclear sheaves:

Definition 5.12
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be an
\ell
-fine map of small v-stacks.

(a)

We define
f_{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
to be the functor
f_{!}:=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}([Y\mathbin{\xleftarrow{\id}}Y\mathbin{\xrightarrow{f}}X],\Lambda)
, where
\D(-,\Lambda)
is the 6-functor formalism from
Theorem

5.11
.

(b)

We define
f^{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)
to be the right adjoint of
f_{!}
.

Remark 5.13
.

The construction of the functor
f_{!}
in
Theorem

5.11
is not very explicit, so we provide a more direct description:

1.

Suppose that
f\colon Y\to X
is quasicompact, compactifiable and representable in spatial diamonds with finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
. Then we define

\displaystyle f_{!}:=(\overline{f}^{/X})_{*}\mathbin{\circ}j_{!},

where
j
denotes the open immersion
j\colon Y\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}\overline{Y}^{/X}
and
j_{!}
is the functor from
Lemma

5.2
.

2.

Suppose that
f\colon Y\to X
is an fdcs map of locally spatial diamonds. Let
\mathcal{I}
be the category of open subsets
V\subseteq Y
which are quasicompact and compactifiable over
X
. Then we have
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)=\varinjlim_{V\in\mathcal{I}}\D_{{\mathrm{nuc}},!}(V,\Lambda)
in the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories and colimit preserving functors, where
\D_{{\mathrm{nuc}},!}
is the functor mapping an inclusion
j\colon V\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}V^{\prime}
to
j_{!}
. We thus need to define
f_{!}
as the functor

\displaystyle f_{!}\mathcal{M}:=\varinjlim_{V\in\mathcal{I}}f_{V!}({\mathcal{M}}|_{V}),

where
f_{V}\colon V\to X
denotes the composition
V\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}Y\to X
. The functors
f_{V!}
were defined in the previous step.

3.

Suppose that
f\colon Y\to X
is an arbitrary fdcs map of small v-stacks. Choose a hypercover
X_{\bullet}\to X
such that all
X_{n}
are locally spatial diamonds and let
f_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}
be the base-change of
f
. Then we have
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X_{n},\Lambda)
and
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)=\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y_{n},\Lambda)
and with this representation we define

\displaystyle f_{!}(\mathcal{M}_{n})_{n}:=(f_{n!}\mathcal{M}_{n})_{n},

where each
f_{n!}
was defined in the previous step. This definition is possible because all
f_{n!}
satisfy arbitrary base-change.

4.

Suppose that
f\colon Y\to X
is an
\ell
-fine map such that
X
admits a map to some strictly totally disconnected space. Pick an fdcs map
g\colon Z\to Y
which admits universal
\ell
-codescent such that
f\mathbin{\circ}g
is fdcs and let
g_{\bullet}\colon Z_{\bullet}\to Y
denote the Čech nerve of
g
. Then for every
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)
we have
\mathcal{M}=\varinjlim_{n\in\Delta}g_{n!}g_{n}^{!}\mathcal{M}
and hence we must define

\displaystyle f_{!}\mathcal{M}:=\varinjlim_{n\in\Delta}(f\mathbin{\circ}g_{n})_{!}g_{n}^{!}\mathcal{M},

where the functors
(f\mathbin{\circ}g_{n})_{!}
and
g_{n}^{!}
were defined in the previous step.

5.

Suppose that
f\colon Y\to X
is a general
\ell
-fine map of small v-stacks. Then we can use the same descent technique as in step 3 to reduce the definition of
f_{!}
to the previous step.

Note that one could attempt to carry out the above construction directly instead of relying on the theory of abstract 6-functor formalisms from
[
10
, §A.5]
. This is possible to some extent, e.g. steps 1, 2 and 3 were carried out in
[
15
, §22]
. This has two downsides though: Firstly, the direct construction will not provide all the higher homotopies one expects the shriek functors to satisfy, so that thorough proofs involving the shriek functors require a lot of diagram checking; secondly, it seems very hard to carry out steps 4 and 5 using this approach.

We have constructed the full 6-functor formalism for nuclear sheaves over a fixed nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
. Sometimes it is useful to also understand how this 6-functor formalism behaves under a change of
\Lambda
. For the following result we denote by
\Ring_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},{\mathrm{nuc}}}
the
\infty
-category of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras.

Proposition 5.14
.

There is a 6-functor formalism

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\Corr(\vStacks\times\Ring_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},{\mathrm{nuc}}}^{\mathrm{op}})_{lfine,all}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty},\qquad(X,\Lambda)\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda),

where
lfine
denotes the class of those maps
(Y,\Lambda^{\prime})\to(X,\Lambda)
where the map
Y\to X
is an
\ell
-fine map of small v-stacks and the map
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. This 6-functor formalism has the following properties:

(i)

For every small v-stack
X
and any map
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras, the induced pullback functor
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime})
is
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
.

(ii)

For every nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
, the restriction of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}
to
\Corr(\vStacks\times\{\Lambda\})_{lfine,all}
coincides with the 6-functor formalism
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)
from
Theorem

5.11
.

Proof.

Using the functor
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\colon\vStacks\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}^{\otimes}
and abstract nonsense involving the usual straightening and unstraightening techniques and the generalized
\infty
-operad
\Mod(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}
from
[
8
, Definition 4.5.1.1]
we can construct a functor

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\vStacks\times\Ring_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},{\mathrm{nuc}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}^{\otimes},\qquad(X,\Lambda)\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)

which satisfies (i) and restricts to the functor from
3.16.(i)
for fixed
\Lambda
(here
\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}^{\otimes}
denotes the
\infty
-category of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories). Now the construction of the desired 6-functor formalism can be carried out in the same way as in
Theorem

5.11
. The only difference occurs at the very beginning of the construction in
Proposition

5.6
, where we additionally need to verify the compatibility of proper pushforward and étale lower shriek with the base-change
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
; this follows immediately from the projection formula.
∎

Corollary 5.15
.

Let
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
be a map of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras and
f\colon Y\to X
an
\ell
-fine map of small v-stacks.

(i)

The functor
f_{!}
commutes naturally with the base-change and the forgetful functor along
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
.

(ii)

The functor
f^{!}
commutes naturally with the forgetful functor along
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The fact that
f_{!}
commutes with the base-change functor
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
follows immediately from proper base-change in the 6-functor formalism from
Proposition

5.14
for the cartesian diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,\Lambda^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Lambda^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Lambda)}

Note that it is really necessary to use
Proposition

5.14
here even though on underlying
\Lambda
-modules the desired commutation of functors reduces to the projection formula. Namely, without the 6-functor formalism from
Proposition

5.14
we do not even get a natural morphism between the functors
f_{!}(-)\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
and
f_{!}(-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime})
from
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)
to
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime})
(only as functors to
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
, but that is not enough). Claim (ii) follows immediately by passing to right adjoints.

It remains to see that
f_{!}
commutes with the forgetful functor along
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
, which by the commutation of
f_{!}
with
-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}
is now a condition rather than an additional datum. Therefore this can be checked along all the steps in the direct computation of
f_{!}
in
Remark

5.13
, which ultimately reduces us to the case that either
f
is an open immersion or proper. In the former case we apply
5.2.(iii)
. In the latter case we have
f_{!}=f_{*}
, so that the claim follows by passing to left adjoints and using
3.17.(i)
.
∎

We finish this section by recording the following formal consequences of any 6-functor formalism, which are useful statements by themselves.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be an
\ell
-fine map of small v-stacks.

(i)

For all
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\Lambda)
there is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\IHom(f_{!}\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M})=f_{*}\IHom(\mathcal{N},f^{!}\mathcal{M}).

(ii)

For all
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
there is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle f^{!}\IHom(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M})=\IHom(f^{*}\mathcal{N},f^{!}\mathcal{M}).

Proof.

This is formal, see e.g.
[
15
, Proposition 23.3]
.
∎

6
Perfect, Dualizable and Overconvergent Sheaves

Fix a prime
\ell\neq p
and a nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
. Having developed a full 6-functor formalism for nuclear
\Lambda
-sheaves on small v-stacks, we now want to study some particular special cases of nuclear sheaves.

We start with dualizable sheaves. The notion of dualizable objects can be defined in any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category:

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. An object
P\in\mathcal{C}
is called
dualizable
if there are an object
P^{*}
, called the
dual
of
P
, and morphisms

\displaystyle\ev_{P}\colon P^{*}\otimes P\to 1,\qquad i_{P}\colon 1\to P\otimes P^{*},

called the
evaluation map
and
coevaluation map
respectively, such that there are homotopy coherent diagrams
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\otimes P^{*}\otimes P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{P}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\ev_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{*}\otimes P\otimes P^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes i_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{P}\otimes\id}

We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dlb}}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
the full subcategory spanned by the dualizable objects in
\mathcal{C}
.

It is easy to see that if
P
is dualizable then the dual
P^{*}
is unique up to unique isomorphism. We will use that fact without further mention below.

Note that by design the notion of dualizability in a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
only depends on the underlying symmetric monoidal
1
-category. In this case one of the first instances where it appears in the literature is in
[
2
, §1]
. If
\mathcal{C}
is closed, i.e. admits an internal hom functor
\IHom
, then there is a different way of defining the dual of an object
P\in\mathcal{C}
by letting
P^{\vee}:=\IHom(P,1)
, where
1\in\mathcal{C}
is the monoidal unit. We say that
P
is
reflexive
if the natural map
P\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}P^{\vee\vee}
is an isomorphism. A natural question is how this notion of duals behaves with respect to dualizability. Here is the answer:

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and
P\in\mathcal{C}
an object. Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

P
is dualizable.

(ii)

P
is reflexive and the natural composed map
P\otimes P^{\vee}\to P^{\vee\vee}\otimes P^{\vee}\to(P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

The natural map
P\otimes P^{\vee}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\IHom(P,P)
is an isomorphism.

(iv)

For all
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
the natural map

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\Hom(M,N\otimes P^{\vee})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\pi_{0}\Hom(M\otimes P,N),

given by sending
f\colon M\to N\otimes P^{\vee}
into the composition

\displaystyle M\otimes P\mathbin{\xrightarrow{f\otimes\id}}N\otimes P^{\vee}\otimes P\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\id\otimes\ev}}N,

is an isomorphism.

If this is the case then
P^{\vee}
is the dual of
P
in the sense of
Definition

6.1
and for all
M\in\mathcal{C}
the natural map

\displaystyle P^{\vee}\otimes M\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\IHom(P,M)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Note that all the statements only depend on the underlying
1
-category of
\mathcal{C}
, so we can assume that
\mathcal{C}
is a
1
-category. Then the equivalence of (i), (ii) and (iv) is shown in
[
2
, Theorem 1.3]
. It goes as follows: The equivalence of (i) and (iv) follows easily by observing that both statements are equivalent to the fact that the functors
-\otimes P
and
-\otimes P^{*}
(resp.
-\otimes P^{\vee}
) are adjoint with the obvious counit. This also proves that necessarily
P^{*}=P^{\vee}
in (i). Note that this implies that condition (iv) equivalently holds with the roles of
P
and
P^{\vee}
swapped. With this known, it is straightforward to see that (iv) implies (ii). To prove that (ii) implies (i), note that we always have the evaluation map
\ev\colon P\otimes P^{\vee}\to 1
and if the isomorphism in (ii) holds then we also get a coevaluation map
i\colon 1=1^{\vee}\to(P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}=P\otimes P^{\vee}
. To show that the necessary triangles commute, we consider the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}
:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\otimes P^{\vee}\otimes P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\vee\vee}\otimes P^{\vee}\otimes P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\vee\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}\otimes P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\ev}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\ev}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

Here the vertical isomorphisms are induced by the isomorphisms in (ii) (where in the middle column the maps act as the identity on the right-hand
P
). The map
\alpha
is the natural map induced by the dualized evaluation map
1=1^{\vee}\to((P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}
(intuitively this singles out the element
A\in(P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}
given as the map
P\otimes P^{\vee}\to 1
,
(x,f)\mapsto f(x)
), so that the left-hand triangle of the diagram commutes by definition of
i
. Also clearly the upper right square commutes. The map
\beta
is the map which by adjunction corresponds to the map

\displaystyle(P\otimes P^{\vee})^{\vee}=\IHom(P\otimes P^{\vee},1)\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\IHom(P,P^{\vee\vee})

(Intuitively it sends a map
A\colon P\times P^{\vee}\to 1
and an element
x\in P
to the element
f\mapsto A(x,f)
in
P^{\vee\vee}
.) One checks immediately that the lower right triangle commutes. It follows that the whole diagram commutes. Moreover, we see that
\beta\mathbin{\circ}\alpha
is the natural map
P\to P^{\vee\vee}
. By the commutativity of the diagram and the fact that
P\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}P^{\vee\vee}
is an isomorphism, we deduce that the composition of the upper two horizontal maps is the identity, as desired. The second diagram in the definition of dualizable maps can be verified similarly (or note that everything is symmetric in
P
and
P^{\vee}
).

We have now shown that (i), (ii) and (iv) are all equivalent. To show that (ii) implies (iii), we simply note that if (ii) holds then

\displaystyle P\otimes P^{\vee}=(P^{\vee}\otimes P)^{\vee}=\IHom(P,P^{\vee\vee})=\IHom(P,P),

as desired. Finally, assume that (iii) is satisfied. Then additionally to the natural evaluation map
\ev\colon P\otimes P^{\vee}\to 1
there is a coevaluation map
1\to\IHom(P,P)=P\otimes P^{\vee}
given by the identity on
P
. One checks that these two maps exhibit
P
as dualizable with dual
P^{\vee}
: The left-hand triangle is obviously commutative; for the right-hand triangle we use the fact that for every
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
the evaluation map
M^{\vee}\otimes M\otimes N^{\vee}\to N^{\vee}
factors over
M^{\vee}\otimes\IHom(N,M)
, where the map from this object to
N^{\vee}
is a special case of the general map

\displaystyle\IHom(X,Y)\otimes\IHom(Y,Z)\to\IHom(X,Z)

for
X,Y,Z\in\mathcal{C}
. Now apply this to
N=M=P
to conclude.

We proved the equivalence of (i), (ii), (iii) and (iv). The final claim follows immediately from (iv).
∎

Remark 6.3
.

We put a lot of care in the proof of
Lemma

6.2
, even though the proof is rather straightforward and the results are not new. The reasons for this are twofold:

1.

We found it surprisingly hard to collect these results from the literature, and we were unable to find a reference where all displayed characterizations of dualizability are stated as cleanly as here.

2.
