computing (continuous) group cohomology.

(ii)

Suppose that
G
is profinite and
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. Then the functor
\Gamma(G,-)
has cohomological dimension
\leq\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G+1
and thus preserves all small colimits.

Proof.

For all claims we can assume
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. To prove the claimed equivalence of
\infty
-categories we note that both
\infty
-categories admit natural left-complete
t
-structures and all the pullback functors in the Čech nerve of the cover
*\to*/G
are
t
-exact, hence by
[
10
, Proposition A.1.2.(ii)]
the claim can be verified on the hearts. The heart of the left-hand category admits a simple description in terms of descent data (cf.
[
10
, Proposition A.1.2.(i)]
), which one easily verifies to be the same as a continuous
G
-representation (here we implicitly use
Corollary

10.2
).

It remains to prove claims (i) and (ii). Claim (i) follows immediately by comparing the associated left adjoints. For claim (ii) we note that if
G
is profinite then
*/G
is qcqs, hence
\Gamma(G,-)
commutes with filtered colimits of static objects. Thus the cohomological dimension of
\Gamma(G,-)
can be checked on
\ell
-adically complete objects (recall that
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
is compactly generated by the
\ell
-adically complete objects
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[G]
), where we can pull the limit
\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}/\ell^{n}\mathcal{M}
out of the cohomology, so that the claim follows from the fact that countable limits have cohomological dimension
1
and that mod
\ell^{n}
the claim follows inductively from the definition and the standard cofiber sequences
M/\ell^{n-1}M\to M/\ell^{n}M\to M/\ell M
.
∎

With
Lemma

10.3
at hand we now study the full subcategory spanned by the nuclear sheaves. The corresponding representations are the continuous representations on nuclear modules:

Definition 10.4
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group. We denote by
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
the full subcategory spanned by those
G
-representations whose underlying
\Lambda
-module is nuclear. The objects of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
are called the
nuclear
G
-representations
over
\Lambda
.

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group. Then there is a natural equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(*/G,\Lambda)=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}.

Moreover, the
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
restricts to a complete
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
. The heart
\mathcal{A}:=(\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG})^{\heartsuit}
is a Grothendieck abelian category which is stable under kernels, cokernels and extensions in the heart of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
. If
\Lambda
is static then there is a natural equivalence
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}=\D^{+}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

The first claim follows immediately from
Lemma

10.3
because on both sides of the claimed equivalence the nuclear sheaves are characterized by those solid sheaves which become nuclear after pullback to
*
(respectively, after forgetting the
G
-action).

That the
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
restricts to a
t
-structure on nuclear representations can be checked on underlying
\Lambda
-modules, i.e. we need to see that the
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
. We can further assume
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. Then the claim boils down to the observation that truncations of Banach
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules are again Banach
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-modules, which follows from the fact that
\ell
-adically complete objects are stable under truncations and that discreteness mod
\ell
is stable under truncations because discretization is
t
-exact (the latter property fails on spatial diamonds, already on profinite sets). The completeness of the
t
-structure follows immediately from the completeness of the
t
-structure of the ambient
\infty
-category
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
.

Now let
\mathcal{A}
denote the heart of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
. It is clear that
\mathcal{A}
is stable under kernels, cokernels and extensions because all of these can be constructed using finite (co)limits and truncations in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
. It is also clearly stable under filtered colimits, hence filtered colimits in
\mathcal{A}
are exact. It is formal that
\mathcal{A}
is a Grothendieck abelian category: By descent,
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is presentable and clearly its
t
-structure is accessible in the sense of
[
8
, Proposition 1.4.4.13]
; thus the claim follows from
[
8
, Remark 1.3.5.23]
.

Now assume that
\Lambda
is static. Then for all profinite sets
S
the solid
G
-representation
(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[G\times S]=\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[G\times S]
is static because
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[G\times S]
is flat over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
(by
Proposition

2.8
this flatness reduces to the flatness of
\mathbb{F}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[G\times S]
over
\mathbb{F}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
which can be deduced easily from the fact that compact
\mathbb{F}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
-modules are stable under kernels and cokernels, cf. the proof of
[
10
, Lemma 2.9.35]
). But
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
is the
\infty
-category of modules over the associative analytic ring
(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[G]
and since all the compact projective generators of this ring are static it follows that
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
is the derived
\infty
-category of its heart. Now consider the natural functor

\displaystyle F\colon\D^{+}(\mathcal{A})\to\D^{+}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}.

It admits a right adjoint
RG
which is the right derived functor of the nuclearization functor
G
on the hearts. We claim that the unit
\id\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}RG\mathbin{\circ}F
is an isomorphism. This can be checked on static representations, i.e. for
M\in\mathcal{A}
we need to see that
M\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}RG(F(M))
is an isomorphism. Let
M\to I^{\bullet}
be an injective resolution of
M=F(M)
in the heart of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
. Then
RG(F(M))
is represented by the complex
G(I^{\bullet})
. On the other hand, this complex clearly also computes
M_{\mathrm{nuc}}
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
. Since
M
is nuclear this implies that this complex is indeed isomorphic to
M
in either derived
\infty
-category. This proves that
F
is fully faithful. The essential image is stable under finite and filtered colimits and contains
\mathcal{A}
and is therefore precisely
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
.
∎

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group with locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension.

(i)

If
\Lambda
is
\ell
-adically complete and
G
is profinite then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is generated under filtered colimits by
\ell
-adically complete nuclear
G
-representations.

(ii)

If
G
is profinite and
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
then the v-pushforward along
*/G\to*
preserves nuclear sheaves and thus restricts to a colimit-preserving functor
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(*/G,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
.

(iii)

If
\Lambda
is static then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is the derived
\infty
-category of its heart.

Proof.

We first prove (i), so assume that
G
is profinite. Let us furthermore assume that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. Then by
10.3.(ii)
the étale site of the stack
*/G
behaves in a very similar way as it does for an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. In particular by the same arguments as in
Section

3
we see that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated by the basic nuclear sheaves, which themselves are sequential colimits of
\ell
-adically complete sheaves. We also get a good description of the
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves on
*/G
in a similar fashion as in
Section

2
and the nuclearization functor
(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}^{BG}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
preserves all small colimits and is bounded. In particular we deduce that every nuclear
G
-representation is a filtered colimit of
\ell
-adically complete nuclear
G
-representations.

To finish the proof of (i) we still need to treat the case that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G=\infty
. But by assumption on
G
there is an open compact subgroup
H\subseteq G
such that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}H<\infty
. The conservative pullback along
*/H\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}*/G
has a left adjoint (the lower shriek functor, see
Lemma

5.2
) on nuclear sheaves. Therefore, since
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BH}
is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated, the same follows for
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
and the
\omega_{1}
-compact generators are the shriek pushforwards along
*/H\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}*/G
of the
\omega_{1}
-compact generators of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BH}
. This implies (i).

We now prove (ii) so assume that
G
is as in the claim. We can assume
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. By
10.3.(ii)
the v-pushforward along
*/G\to*
preserves small colimits of solid sheaves, hence by (i) we only need to show that this v-pushforward preserves Banach sheaves. This in turn reduces to showing that it preserves étale sheaves, i.e. that the continuous group cohomology of a discrete representation is discrete. This can for example be checked by a direct computation of group cohomology, cf.
[
10
, Proposition 3.4.6]
.

We now prove (ii), so assume that
\Lambda
is static and let
\mathcal{A}
be the heart of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
. By
Lemma

10.5
we have
\D^{+}(\mathcal{A})=\D^{+}_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
and
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is left-complete. It is therefore enough to show that
\D(\mathcal{A})
is left-complete. For this it is enough to show that countable products have finite cohomological dimension in
\D(\mathcal{A})
(e.g. by adapting the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 1.2.1.19]
). Equivalently we need to show that countable products in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
have finite cohomological dimension. This can be checked after pullback along any étale cover of
*/G
(such a pullback is
t
-exact and preserves limits of nuclear sheaves by the existence of the left adjoint lower shriek functor), so we can replace
G
by any open subgroup. In particular we can assume that
G
is profinite and
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. Limits in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
can be computed as the composition of the limit in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}^{BG}
and the nuclearization functor. By the proof of (i) nuclearization has finite cohomological dimension, hence so do countable products in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
.
∎

We have acquired a clear understanding of the connection of nuclear sheaves on classifying stacks and nuclear representations. We now study the geometry of these classifying stacks from an
\ell
-cohomological viewpoint. Our first goal is to show that essentially all maps of classifying stacks that appear in practice are
\ell
-fine.

Proposition 10.7
.

Let
G
be a profinite group and let
M,N\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
be static nuclear
G
-representations such that
N
is
\ell
-adically complete. Then

\displaystyle\Ext^{k}(M,N)=0\qquad\text{for all $k>\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G+3$}.

Proof.

We can assume that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
because otherwise there is nothing to prove. Let
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(M,N)
denote the internal hom of
M
and
N
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
. Then for the spectra-enriched Hom from
M
to
N
we have
\Hom(M,N)=\Gamma(G,\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(M,N))
. By
10.3.(ii)
it is therefore enough to show that
\IExt^{k}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(M,N)=0
for
k>2
. Note that the solid internal hom is computed on the underlying
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
-modules (it agrees with the pro-étale internal hom and is thus preserved under pullback along the pro-étale map
*\to*/G
). We can therefore ignore the group action from now on and simply assume that
M,N\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
with
N
being
\ell
-adically complete. By pulling out the limit
N=\varprojlim_{n}N/\ell^{n}N
and using the fact that countable limits have cohomological dimension 1, we reduce to showing that
\IExt^{k}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(M,N/\ell^{n}N)=0
for
i>1
. In other words, from now on we can assume that
N
is a discrete
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}
-module. Then
\IHom_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(M,N)=\IHom_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}},{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(M/\ell^{n}M,N)
. Since
M/\ell^{n}M
is discrete, we can equivalently write it as
M/\ell^{n}M=M_{0}/\ell^{n}M_{0}
, where
M_{0}
is the underlying discrete abelian group of
M
. We can pick a short exact sequence
0\to\bigoplus_{J}{\mathbb{Z}}\to\bigoplus_{I}{\mathbb{Z}}\to M_{0}\to 0
for some sets
I
and
J
. Thus
M_{0}/\ell^{n}M_{0}
admits a resolution of length 2 in terms of direct sums of copies of
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}
. Since products are exact in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}})
it follows immediately that
\IHom_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}},{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(M_{0}/\ell^{n}M_{0},N)
is concentrated in cohomological degrees
0
and
1
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
G
be a profinite group with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. Then the map
*\to*/G
is fdcs and admits universal
\ell
-codescent.

Proof.

It is clear that the map
*\to*/G
is fdcs because it is proper and pro-étale. To prove universal
\ell
-codescent we follow our argument in
[
5
, Lemma 3.11]
, so the reader is encouraged to have a look at loc. cit. for more details. Pick any small v-stack
X
with a map
X\to*/G
and let
Y:=X\times_{*/G}*
. We denote
q\colon Y\to X
the base-change of
*\to*/G
and
q_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet}\to X
the associated Čech nerve. Then we need to show that the natural functor

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}^{!}(Y_{n},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

is an equivalence. By employing Lurie’s Beck-Chevalley condition (see
[
8
, Corollary 4.7.5.3]
) this reduces to the following claim:

1.

The functor
q^{!}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is conservative and preserves geometric realizations of
q^{!}
-split simplicial objects in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.

To prove this we apply ideas of Mathew
[
12
]
. We first note that it is enough to show the following claim:

1.

Let
\langle q_{*}q^{!}\rangle\subseteq\Fun(\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}),\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}))
be the full subcategory generated by
q_{*}q^{!}
under finite (co)limits, compositions and retracts; then
\langle q_{*}q^{!}\rangle
contains the identity functor.

One checks easily that (b) implies (a): It follows easily from (b) that the functor
q_{*}q^{!}
is conservative, hence so is
q^{!}
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
is any
q^{!}
-split simplicial object in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
then it is also
q_{*}q^{!}
-split and by (b) it follows that it is split; then of course its geometric realization commutes with
q^{!}
.

It remains to prove (b). We compute
q_{*}q^{!}=\IHom(q_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},-)
, so (b) reduces to the claim that
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
can be generated using finite (co)limits, retracts and tensor products from
q_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. In other words, using Mathew’s notion of descendable algebras (see
[
12
, Definition 3.18, Proposition 3.20]
) the claim (b) boils down to:

1.

The algebra object
q_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
admits descent.

Let us denote
q_{0}\colon*\to*/G
the canonical projection, so that
q
is a base-change of
q_{0}
along the map
f\colon X\to*/G
. The pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(*/G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is symmetric monoidal and sends
q_{0*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
to
q_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
(by proper base-change), so it is enough to show (c) for
q_{0}
. Thus from now on we assume
X=*/G
. By
Proposition

10.6
the proof of (c) reduces to the following claim:

1.

The algebra object
\cts(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
admits descent.

Let
d:=\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G+3
and consider the cofiber sequence

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\Tot_{d}(\cts(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{\otimes\bullet+1})\to X

in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{BG}
, where
\Tot_{d}
denotes the
d
-truncated totalization of a cosimplicial object. By descent we have
\Tot(\cts(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{\otimes\bullet+1})
and thus
X
is concentrated in cohomological degrees
\geq d
. But then it follows from
Proposition

10.7
that the connecting map
X\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[1]
must be zero, which implies that
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
is a retract of
\Tot_{d}(\cts(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})^{\otimes\bullet+1})
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group which has locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension. Then:

(i)

The natural projection
*/G\to*
is
\ell
-fine.

(ii)

If
G
is profinite and has finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension then the map
*/G\to*
is
\ell
-cohomologically proper.

Proof.

For (i) we can replace
G
by any compact open subgroup because the property of being
\ell
-fine is étale local on the source (see
Lemma

5.10
). We can thus assume that
G
is profinite with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. In this case the map
*/G\to*
is covered by the map
*\to*/G
which is fdcs and admits universal
\ell
-codescent by
Lemma

10.8
, so that
*\to*/G
is
\ell
-fine by definition.

It remains to prove (ii) so assume that
G
is as in the claim. First note that the diagonal of
f\colon*/G\to*
has fiber
G
and is thus proper, so that
f
is 1-separated. By
10.6.(ii)

f_{*}
preserves all small colimits, so we can argue as in
[
5
, Corollary 3.12]
to deduce that the natural map
f_{!}\to f_{*}
is an isomorphism, implying
\ell
-cohomological properness of
f
.
∎

We have established the fact that for nice enough locally profinite groups
G
the classifying stack
*/G
is
\ell
-fine, so in particular we have the full 6-functor formalism for nuclear sheaves on classfying stacks at our disposal. In order to compute shriek functors, it is very useful to know that in practice the classifying stack
*/G
is even
\ell
-cohomologically smooth. We have already worked this out for
p
-adic sheaves in
[
5
, §3.2]
and it works the same way
\ell
-adically. For the convenience of the reader we present the main definitions and results.

Definition 10.10
.

(a)

Let
G
be a profinite group with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. We say that
G
is
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
d
if it satisfies the following conditions:

1.

H^{k}(G,M)
is finite for all
k\geq 0
and all
G
-representations
M
on finite
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
-vector spaces.

2.

The solid
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
-vector space
\Gamma(G,\mathbb{F}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[G])
is invertible and concentrated in cohomological degree
d
.

(b)

Let
G
be a locally profinite group with locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension. We say that
G
is
virtually
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
d
if there is some compact open subgroup
H\subseteq G
such that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}H<\infty
and
H
is
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
d
.

Examples 10.11
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group.

(a)

If
G
is an
\ell
-adic Lie group of dimension
d
then
G
is virtually
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
d
. This follows from results of Lazard
[
6
]
, see
[
5
, Theorem 3.18]
.

(b)

If
G
is locally pro-
p
then it is virtually
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
0
. Indeed, if
G
is pro-
p
then
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G=0
by
[
13
, Corollary III.3.3.7]
, i.e.
\Gamma(G,-)
is acyclic on
\ell
-torsion representations of
G
; it follows easily that
G
is
\ell
-Poincaré of dimension
0
.

In the following when we say that a locally profinite group
G
is virtually
\ell
-Poincaré then we implicitly always mean that
G
has locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension.

Lemma 10.12
.

Let
G
be a profinite group with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. Then
G
satisfies condition (i) of
10.10.(a)
if and only if
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\in\D_{\mathrm{et}}(*/G,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
is dualizable over
*
.

Proof.

We have a cartesian square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/(G\times G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

By
Proposition

7.7
the constant sheaf
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\in\D_{\mathrm{et}}(*/G,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
is
f
-dualizable if and only if the natural map
\pi_{1}^{*}f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\to\pi_{2}^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
is an isomorphism. For every compact open subgroup
H\subseteq G
let
g_{H}\colon*/(H\times H)\to*
and
h_{H}\colon*/(H\times H)\to*/(G\times G)
be the natural maps. Then the collection of functors
g_{H*}h_{H}^{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(*/(G\times G),\mathbb{F}_{\ell})\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(*,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
is conservative (this is a reformulation of the fact that a smooth
(G\times G)
-representation is determined by its
(H\times H)
-invariants for all
H
). Consequently
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
is
f
-dualizable if and only if for all
H
the map

\displaystyle g_{H*}h_{H}^{*}\pi_{1}^{*}f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\to g_{H*}h_{H}^{*}\pi_{2}^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}

(10.12.1)

is an isomorphism. We compute both sides in the case that
H=G
; for general
H
one gets the same terms but with
G
replaced by
H
. By
10.9.(ii)
all the appearing maps are
\ell
-cohomologically proper, so that in particular their pushforwards satisfy the projection formula and base-change. We get

\displaystyle g_{G*}h_{G}^{*}\pi_{1}^{*}f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=f_{*}\pi_{1*}\pi_{1}^{*}f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=f_{*}(f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\otimes\pi_{1*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell})=f_{*}(f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\otimes f^{*}f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell})=

\displaystyle\qquad=f_{*}f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}\otimes f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=(f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell})^{\vee}\otimes f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell},

and similarly we have

\displaystyle g_{H*}h_{H}^{*}\pi_{2}^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=f_{*}\pi_{2*}\pi_{2}^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=f_{*}\IHom(\pi_{2*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell},\mathbb{F}_{\ell})=f_{*}\IHom(f^{*}f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell},\mathbb{F}_{\ell})=

\displaystyle\qquad=\IHom(f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell},f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}).

We deduce that
Eq.

10.12.1
is an isomorphism (for
H=G
) if and only if
f_{*}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}=\Gamma(G,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
is dualizable. If we now let
H
vary we find that
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
is
f
-dualizable if and only if
\Gamma(H,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
is dualizable for all compact open subgroups
H\subseteq G
. Since every finite
G
-representation is a retract of a finite (co)limit of the generators
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[G/H]
, we deduce that this condition is indeed equivalent to condition (i) of
10.10.(a)
.
∎

Theorem 10.13
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group with locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension. Then the map
*/G\to*
is
\ell
-cohomologically smooth (of pure dimension
d/2
) if and only if
G
is virtually
\ell
-Poincaré (of dimension
d
).

Proof.

By
10.9.(i)
the map
f\colon*/G\to*
is
\ell
-fine so it makes sense to speak of
\ell
-cohomological smoothness. Since
\ell
-cohomological smoothness is étale local on the source we can assume that
G
is profinite with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}G<\infty
. By
Lemma

10.12
we can assume that
G
satisfies condition (i) of
10.10.(a)
and we only need to show that then
G
satisfies condition (ii) of that definition if and only if
f^{!}\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
is invertible. This is a formal computation for which we refer the reader to our argument in
[
5
, Proposition 3.14.(ii)]
.
∎

With a good understanding of the classifying stacks at hand, it now makes sense to introduce admissible representations in the following form:

Definition 10.14
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group with locally finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension. A nuclear
G
-representation
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
is called
admissible
if it is relatively dualizable over
*
(when viewed as a nuclear
\Lambda
-module on
*/G
).

The above definition of admissible representations falls naturally out of the geometric setting, but if
\Lambda
is not discrete then it seems to be a notion which has not been studied much in the literature yet. We do not attempt a full study of admissible nuclear representations, but we can at least state some easy formal consequences of the definition:

Proposition 10.15
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite virtually
\ell
-Poincaré group and let
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}
be a nuclear
G
-representation.

(i)

Let
i_{1},i_{2}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{B(G\times G)}
denote the two inflation operators. Then
M
is admissible if and only if the natural map

\displaystyle i_{1}(M^{\vee})\otimes i_{2}(M)\to\IHom(i_{1}(M),i_{2}(M))

is an isomorphism of nuclear
(G\times G)
-representations.

(ii)

If
M
is admissible then it is reflexive, i.e. the map
M\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}M^{\vee\vee}
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

Let
H\subseteq G
be an open subgroup. Then
M
is admissible as a
G
-representation if and only if it is admissible as an
H
-representation.

(iv)

If
M
is admissible then
\Gamma(H,M)
is dualizable for every compact open subgroup
H\subseteq G
with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}H<\infty
. If
\Lambda
is discrete then the converse of this statement holds.

(v)

Let
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
be a map of nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebras. If
M
is admissible then
M\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda^{\prime})^{BG}
is admissible.

(vi)

Suppose that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
and that
M
is bounded and
\ell
-adically complete. Then
M
is admissible if and only if
M/\ell M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda/\ell\Lambda)^{BG}
is admissible.

Proof.

For (i), note that
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
are just the pullbacks along the two projections
*/(G\times G)\to*/G
. Using also the fact that both are
\ell
-cohomologically smooth by
Theorem

10.13
we easily reduce the claim to
Proposition

7.7
. Part (ii) is a special case of the last part of
Proposition

7.7
. For (iii), note that
*/H\to*/G
is an étale and hence
\ell
-cohomologically smooth cover, hence the claim is a special case of
Proposition

8.6
. For (iv), by
10.9.(ii)
the stack
*/H
is
\ell
-cohomologically proper over
*
, hence the first part of the claim is a special case of
Proposition

9.10
. If
\Lambda
is discrete then the family of functors
\Gamma(H,-)\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)^{BG}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
is conservative, so one can argue as in the proof of
Lemma

10.12
to get the second part of (iv). Claims (v) and (vi) are special cases of
Proposition

7.14
.
∎
